                    \n─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n## Repository files navigation\n* [README][131]\n* [Contributing][132]\n* [License][133]\n\n# 🤖 Ralph Wiggum Workflow for Kiro CLI\n\n> **A comprehensive guide to autonomous AI development using the Ralph Wiggum technique with Kiro CLI.**\n\n[[Kiro CLI]][134] [[Autonomous]][135] [[License]][136]\n\n## 📑 Table of Contents\n* [What is Ralph Wiggum?][137]\n* [What is Lisa?][138]\n* [How to Set Up][139]\n* [The Workflow][140]\n* [Phase 1: Clarify (or Lisa)][141]\n* [Phase 2: Plan][142]\n* [Phase 3: Execute][143]\n* [Templates][144]\n* [Quick Start][145]\n* [Differences from Claude Code][146]\n* [Troubleshooting][147]\n* [Philosophy Recap][148]\n* [Sources][149]\n\n## 🧠 What is Ralph Wiggum?\n\nRalph Wiggum is an autonomous development technique created by **Geoffrey Huntley**. At its core, it's simple:\n\nwhile :; do cat PROMPT.md | kiro-cli chat --no-interactive -a; done\n\nA bash loop that repeatedly feeds the same prompt to an AI agent until the task is complete.\n\n### Philosophy\n\n> *\"The technique is **deterministically bad in an undeterministic world**.\"*\n* When Ralph fails, you don't blame the tools — **you tune the prompts**\n* Failures are predictable and informative\n* Success requires faith in eventual consistency\n* Each failure teaches you what \"signs\" (guardrails) to add\n\n### The Playground Metaphor\n\nFrom Huntley:\n\n> *\"Ralph is very good at making playgrounds, but he comes home bruised because he fell off the slide, so one then tunes Ralph by adding a sign next to the slide saying 'SLIDE DOWN, DON'T JUMP, LOOK AROUND,' and Ralph is more likely to look and see the sign.\"*\n\nEventually, the prompt has enough guardrails that Ralph works reliably.\n\n## 💡 What is Lisa?\n\n**Lisa plans. Ralph does.**\n\nLisa is an interactive specification interview workflow adapted from [blencorp/lisa][150] for Kiro CLI. Lisa conducts in-depth feature interviews to generate comprehensive, implementable specifications.\n\n### Why Use Lisa?\n\nBased on the technique by [@trq212][151]:\n\n> *\"My favorite way to use Claude Code to build large features is spec based. Start with a minimal spec or prompt and ask Claude to interview you using the AskUserQuestion tool about literally anything: technical implementation, UI & UX, concerns, tradeoffs, etc. Then make a new session to execute the spec.\"*\n\n### Lisa's Approach\n* **Probing Questions**: Instead of basic \"what should it do?\", Lisa asks \"How should X handle Y when Z fails?\"\n* **Comprehensive Coverage**: Technical implementation, UX, edge cases, trade-offs, and verification\n* **Structured Output**: Generates markdown specs, JSON user stories, and progress tracking files\n* **Ralph-Ready**: Specs are designed to be consumed by Ralph for autonomous implementation\n\n### When to Use Lisa vs Ralph-Clarify\n\n─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nUse Lisa When...                                     │Use Ralph-Clarify When...                             \n─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nBuilding a new feature from scratch                  │Clarifying requirements for an existing system        \n─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nNeed detailed technical specifications               │Need a quick requirements gathering session           \n─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nWant structured user stories with acceptance criteria│Want a flexible requirements document                 \n─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nPlanning to use Ralph for implementation             │Planning to implement manually or with custom workflow\n─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n### Lisa Workflow\n\n`┌──────────────────────────────────────┐\n│         LISA SPECIFICATION           │\n│                                      │\n│  kiro-cli chat --agent lisa-plan     │\n│                                      │\n│  📋 Interactive interview            │\n│  → Probing questions                 │\n│  → Draft updated continuously        │\n│  → Say \"done\" to finalize            │\n│  → Output: Complete spec             │\n└──────────────────────────────────────┘\n              │\n              ▼\n┌──────────────────────────────────────┐\n│    Generated Specification Files     │\n│                                      │\n│  • docs/specs/{feature}.md           │\n│  • docs/specs/{feature}.json         │\n│  • docs/specs/{feature}-progress.txt │\n└──────────────────────────────────────┘\n              │\n              ▼\n┌──────────────────────────────────────┐\n│      RALPH IMPLEMENTATION            │\n│                                      │\n│  Use Ralph to implement the spec     │\n│  (See Phase 3: Execute)              │\n└──────────────────────────────────────┘\n`\n\nSee [examples/lisa-workflow-example.md][152] for a complete walkthrough.\n\n## 🔧 How to Set Up\n\n### Prerequisites\n\n────────────┬───────────────────────────────────\nRequirement │Description                        \n────────────┼───────────────────────────────────\n**Kiro CLI**│Already installed and authenticated\n────────────┼───────────────────────────────────\n**Git**     │For state tracking and memory      \n────────────┴───────────────────────────────────\n\n### Step 1: Install Kiro CLI\n\nIf you haven't already installed Kiro CLI:\n\n# macOS & Linux\ncurl -fsSL https://cli.kiro.dev/install | bash\n\n# Or with Homebrew (macOS)\nbrew install kiro-cli\n\n# Verify installation\nkiro-cli --version\n\n> **⚠️ Important Note**: This workflow is an adaptation of the original Ralph Wiggum technique (designed for Claude Code) to work with Kiro CLI. The Kiro CLI agent system and commands may differ from those shown here. You may need to adjust the agent configurations in `.kiro/agents/` to match your Kiro CLI version's capabilities. Refer to [Kiro CLI documentation][153] for the latest agent configuration format.\n\n### Step 2: Clone this Repository\n\ngit clone https://github.com/RichardScottOZ/ralph-kiro.git\ncd ralph-kiro\n\n### Step 3: Set Up a New Project\n\n# Copy the ralph-kiro setup to your project\n./setup-ralph.sh my-project\n\n# Or manually:\nmkdir my-project\ncd my-project\ngit init\nmkdir -p .kiro/agents\ncp -r /path/to/ralph-kiro/.kiro/agents/* .kiro/agents/\ncp /path/to/ralph-kiro/templates/* .\n\n### Project Structure After Setup\n\n`my-project/\n├── .git/                      # Git repo (required)\n├── .kiro/\n│   └── agents/\n│       ├── ralph-clarify.yaml # Phase 1: Requirements discovery\n│       ├── ralph-plan.yaml    # Phase 2: Generate execution files\n│       ├── lisa-plan.yaml     # Alternative: Lisa spec interview\n│       └── ralph-execute.yaml # Phase 3: Execute tasks\n├── PROMPT.md                  # Ralph's instructions (generated by plan)\n├── TODO.md                    # Task checklist (generated by plan)\n├── clarify-session.md         # Requirements (from ralph-clarify)\n├── docs/\n│   └── specs/                 # Lisa specifications (if using Lisa)\n└── src/                       # Your code (created during execution)\n`\n\n## 🔄 The Workflow\n\n### Option A: Ralph Workflow (Original)\n\n`┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                           PHASE 1: CLARIFY                               │\n│                                                                          │\n│   Command: kiro-cli chat --agent ralph-clarify                          │\n│                                                                          │\n│   📋 40-70 questions via interactive chat                                │\n│   → Exhaustive requirements discovery                                    │\n│   → Output: clarify-session.md                                           │\n└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n                                    │\n                                    ▼\n┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                            PHASE 2: PLAN                                 │\n│                                                                          │\n│   Command: kiro-cli chat --agent ralph-plan                             │\n│                                                                          │\n│   📝 Convert requirements to actionable tasks                            │\n│   → Create PROMPT.md (execution instructions + guardrails)               │\n│   → Create TODO.md (prioritized task checklist)                          │\n└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n                                    │\n                                    ▼\n┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                          PHASE 3: EXECUTE                                    │\n│                                                                              │\n│   Command: while :; do cat PROMPT.md | kiro-cli chat --no-interactive -a; done    │\n│                                                                              │\n│   🔁 Ralph loop until complete                                               │\n│   → Pick task → Complete → Mark done → Repeat                                │\n│   → Output: Working software                                                 │\n└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n`\n\n### Option B: Lisa + Ralph Workflow (Recommended for Features)\n\n`┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                      LISA: SPECIFICATION                                 │\n│                                                                          │\n│   Command: kiro-cli chat --agent lisa-plan                              │\n│                                                                          │\n│   📋 In-depth feature interview                                          │\n│   → Probing questions about implementation                               │\n│   → Draft spec updated continuously                                      │\n│   → Output: Complete spec with user stories                              │\n└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n                                    │\n                                    ▼\n┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                    GENERATED SPECIFICATION                               │\n│                                                                          │\n│   Files: docs/specs/{feature}.md, .json, -progress.txt                  │\n│   → Markdown specification                                               │\n│   → JSON user stories                                                    │\n│   → Progress tracking file                                               │\n└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n                                    │\n                                    ▼\n┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                     RALPH: IMPLEMENTATION                                    │\n│                                                                              │\n│   Create PROMPT.md referencing the spec                                      │\n│   Command: while :; do cat PROMPT.md | kiro-cli chat --no-interactive -a; done    │\n│                                                                              │\n│   🔁 Ralph implements spec until complete                                    │\n│   → Implements user stories                                                  │\n│   → Verifies acceptance criteria                                             │\n│   → Output: Working software                                                 │\n└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n`\n\n## 📋 Phase 1: Clarify (or Lisa)\n\nYou have two options for the requirements/specification phase:\n\n### Option A: Ralph-Clarify (General Requirements)\n\nThe clarify phase uses interactive questioning to gather **40-70 questions** covering all aspects of the project.\n\n#### Why This Matters\n\n> *\"The loop pressure overrides the natural tendency to stop asking after 3-5 questions.\"*\n\n#### Question Categories\n\n──────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nCategory              │Questions to Cover                                              \n──────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n**Core Requirements** │What must this do? What's MVP? What's out of scope?             \n──────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n**Users & Context**   │Who uses this? What's their skill level? What constraints exist?\n──────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n**Technical Choices** │Language? Framework? Database? Dependencies?                    \n──────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n**Integration Points**│What systems does this connect to? What data flows in/out?      \n──────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n**Edge Cases**        │What happens when things fail? Boundary conditions?             \n──────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n**Quality Attributes**│Performance requirements? Security considerations?              \n──────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n**Existing Patterns** │How do similar things work in this codebase? Conventions?       \n──────────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n**Preferences**       │Strong opinions on approach? Tradeoffs?                         \n──────────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n#### Running the Clarify Phase\n\n# Start the clarify agent\nkiro-cli chat --agent ralph-clarify\n\n# In the chat, describe your project:\n# \"I want to build a REST API for task management\"\n\n# The agent will ask you 40-70 questions\n# Answer them thoroughly\n# Results are saved to clarify-session.md\n\n#### Output: `clarify-session.md`\n\n# Discovery: Task Management API\n\nStarted: 2024-01-10\n\n## Questions Asked\n1. What authentication method? → JWT\n2. What database? → PostgreSQL\n3. ...\n\n## Answers Received\n- Authentication: JWT with refresh tokens\n- Database: PostgreSQL with Prisma ORM\n- ...\n\n## Emerging Requirements\n- Must support 1000 concurrent users\n- API must be RESTful with OpenAPI spec\n- All endpoints require authentication except /health\n- ...\n\n### Option B: Lisa (Feature Specifications)\n\n**Recommended for new features.** Lisa conducts a specification interview with probing questions to generate a complete, implementable spec.\n\n#### Running the Lisa Phase\n\n# Start the Lisa agent\nkiro-cli chat --agent lisa-plan\n\n# In the chat, describe your feature:\n# \"I want to spec out a user authentication system\"\n\n# Lisa will ask probing questions about:\n# - Technical implementation details\n# - User experience and edge cases\n# - Trade-offs and concerns\n# - Implementation phases\n# - Verification criteria\n\n# Say \"done\" or \"finalize\" when ready to complete\n\n#### What Makes Lisa Different\n\nUnlike Ralph-Clarify which asks general requirements questions, Lisa asks **non-obvious, probing questions**:\n\n──────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nRalph-Clarify Might Ask       │Lisa Asks Instead                                                                                                                                \n──────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\"What authentication methods?\"│\"What authentication methods, and what's driving that choice - user preference, existing infrastructure, or security requirements?\"              \n──────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\"What should happen on error?\"│\"When login fails due to network issues, should we retry automatically, show a retry button, or redirect to an offline mode?\"                    \n──────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\"What are the requirements?\"  │\"How should the system handle concurrent login attempts from the same user? Should we invalidate old sessions or allow multiple active sessions?\"\n──────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n#### Output Files\n\nLisa generates three files in `docs/specs/`:\n1. **`{feature-slug}.md`** - Comprehensive markdown specification\n2. **`{feature-slug}.json`** - Structured JSON with user stories and acceptance criteria\n3. **`{feature-slug}-progress.txt`** - Progress tracking file for Ralph\n\n**Example:** For \"user authentication system\":\n* `docs/specs/user-authentication-system.md`\n* `docs/specs/user-authentication-system.json`\n* `docs/specs/user-authentication-system-progress.txt`\n\nSee [examples/lisa-workflow-example.md][154] for a complete walkthrough.\n\n## 📝 Phase 2: Plan\n\nConvert the clarify session (or Lisa spec) into actionable instructions.\n\n### Running the Plan Phase\n\n#### From Ralph-Clarify Output\n\n# Start the plan agent\nkiro-cli chat --agent ralph-plan\n\n# Tell it to read clarify-session.md and generate execution files\n# It will create PROMPT.md and TODO.md\n\n#### From Lisa Spec\n\nIf you used Lisa, create a PROMPT.md that references the spec:\n\n# PROMPT.md\n\n## Project\nImplement the {feature name} as specified in docs/specs/{feature-slug}.md\n\n## Requirements\nRead the full specification at docs/specs/{feature-slug}.md\n\nFollow the implementation phases defined in the spec.\n\n## Instructions\n1. Read the spec file for complete requirements\n2. Implement one user story at a time from the JSON spec\n3. After each story, run verification commands\n4. Update the JSON spec's \"passes\" field to true when complete\n5. Continue until all user stories pass\n\n## Completion\nWhen all user stories have \"passes\": true and all tests pass, output: DONE\n\n### Generated Files\n\n**PROMPT.md** — Execution instructions with guardrails\n**TODO.md** — Prioritized task checklist with HARD STOP checkpoints\n\n## 🚀 Phase 3: Execute\n\nRun the Ralph loop until complete.\n\n### Basic Loop\n\n# Simple bash loop\nwhile :; do cat PROMPT.md | kiro-cli chat --no-interactive -a; done\n\n### Enhanced Loop with Completion Detection\n\n# Loop that exits when \"DONE\" is detected\nwhile :; do \n  output=$(cat PROMPT.md | kiro-cli chat --no-interactive -a)\n  echo \"$output\"\n  if echo \"$output\" | grep -q \"DONE\"; then\n    echo \"Task completed!\"\n    break\n  fi\ndone\n\n### Loop with Max Iterations\n\n# Safe loop with iteration limit\nmax_iterations=50\niteration=0\n\nwhile [ $iteration -lt $max_iterations ]; do\n  echo \"Iteration $((iteration + 1))/$max_iterations\"\n  output=$(cat PROMPT.md | kiro-cli chat --no-interactive -a)\n  echo \"$output\"\n\n  if echo \"$output\" | grep -q \"DONE\"; then\n    echo \"Task completed at iteration $((iteration + 1))!\"\n    break\n  fi\n\n  iteration=$((iteration + 1))\ndone\n\nif [ $iteration -eq $max_iterations ]; then\n  echo \"Reached max iterations without completion\"\nfi\n\n### What Happens During Execution\n\n`Iteration 1: Kiro reads PROMPT.md → Picks Task 1 → Implements → Commits\n     ↓\nIteration 2: Kiro reads PROMPT.md → Sees Task 1 done → Picks Task 2 → Implements → Commits\n     ↓\nIteration 3: ...\n     ↓\nIteration N: All tasks done → Kiro outputs \"DONE\" → Loop exits\n`\n\nEach iteration:\n* Sees the same `PROMPT.md`\n* But files have changed (previous work persists)\n* Git history shows progress\n* `TODO.md` reflects completed tasks\n\n## 📄 Templates\n\n### Minimal PROMPT.md (Start Here)\n\n# PROMPT.md\n\nBuild [WHAT YOU'RE BUILDING].\n\nRequirements:\n- [Requirement 1]\n- [Requirement 2]\n- [Requirement 3]\n\nTrack progress in TODO.md. Commit after each completed item.\n\nWhen all requirements are implemented and tests pass, output: DONE\n\n### PROMPT.md with Guardrails\n\n# PROMPT.md\n\n## Task\n[Description of what to build]\n\n## Requirements\nRead clarify-session.md for full requirements.\n\n## Instructions\n1. Read TODO.md for current tasks\n2. Pick highest priority incomplete task\n3. Read any files before editing them\n4. Implement the task\n5. Run tests\n6. If tests fail, fix before continuing\n7. Mark complete in TODO.md\n8. Commit with clear message\n9. Repeat until all tasks done\n\n## Signs (Guardrails)\n- Always read before editing\n- Never skip failing tests\n- Don't refactor unrelated code\n- Keep commits focused\n- Update TODO.md immediately after completing tasks\n\n## Completion\nWhen TODO.md shows all tasks complete and tests pass, output: DONE\n\n### TODO.md Template\n\n# TODO\n\n## Critical (Must Have)\n- [ ] Task 1\n- [ ] Task 2\n- [ ] **HARD STOP** - Verify core flow works end-to-end\n\n## Important (Should Have)\n- [ ] Task 3\n- [ ] Task 4\n\n## Nice to Have\n- [ ] Task 5\n- [ ] Task 6\n\n---\n## Completed\n- [x] Example completed task\n\n## ⚡ Quick Start\n\n# 1. Set up a new project\n./setup-ralph.sh my-awesome-project\ncd my-awesome-project\n\n# 2. Phase 1: Clarify - gather requirements\nkiro-cli chat --agent ralph-clarify\n# Describe your project and answer 40-70 questions\n\n# 3. Phase 2: Plan - convert requirements to execution files\nkiro-cli chat --agent ralph-plan\n# Agent reads clarify-session.md and generates PROMPT.md and TODO.md\n\n# 4. Phase 3: Execute - run the autonomous loop\nwhile :; do \n  output=$(cat PROMPT.md | kiro-cli chat --no-interactive -a)\n  echo \"$output\"\n  if echo \"$output\" | grep -q \"DONE\"; then\n    break\n  fi\ndone\n\n## 🔄 Differences from Claude Code\n\nThis implementation adapts the original Ralph Wiggum workflow from Claude Code to Kiro CLI:\n\n────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────\nFeature                 │Claude Code                          │Kiro CLI (This Repo)                   \n────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────\n**Plugin System**       │`/ralph-loop` command                │Bash loop with `kiro-cli chat`         \n────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────\n**Commands**            │Custom slash commands                │Kiro agents in `.kiro/agents/`         \n────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────\n**Clarify Phase**       │`/ralph-clarify` with AskUserQuestion│Interactive agent chat                 \n────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────\n**Plan Phase**          │`/ralph-plan` command                │Agent reads session and generates files\n────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────\n**Execute Phase**       │`/ralph-loop` with completion promise│Bash loop with grep for \"DONE\"         \n────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────\n**Configuration**       │`.claude/commands/*.md`              │`.kiro/agents/*.yaml`                  \n────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────\n**Loop Control**        │Built-in max iterations              │Manual bash loop control               \n────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────\n**Completion Detection**│`<promise>` tag                      │Plain text \"DONE\" with grep            \n────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────\n\n### Key Adaptations\n1. **Agents instead of Commands**: Kiro CLI uses agent configurations instead of slash commands\n2. **Bash Loop**: Manual bash loop instead of built-in `/ralph-loop` command\n3. **Non-interactive Mode**: Use `--no-interactive -a` (`--trust-all-tools`) for autonomous execution\n4. **Grep for Completion**: Detect completion by grepping output for \"DONE\"\n5. **Manual Iteration Limit**: Build iteration limits into the bash loop\n\n## 🛠️ Troubleshooting\n\n### Loop Runs Forever\n\n**Problem**: The loop doesn't detect completion\n\n**Solutions**:\n* Ensure PROMPT.md explicitly tells the agent to output \"DONE\"\n* Check that the completion string matches exactly\n* Add a max iterations limit to your loop\n\n### Agent Doesn't Follow Instructions\n\n**Problem**: Agent skips tasks or doesn't follow PROMPT.md\n\n**Solutions**:\n* Add more specific guardrails to PROMPT.md\n* Break down large tasks into smaller, clearer subtasks\n* Review completed iterations and add \"signs\" for common failures\n\n### Kiro CLI Authentication Issues\n\n**Problem**: CLI asks for authentication repeatedly\n\n**Solutions**:\n\n# Re-authenticate\nkiro-cli configure\n\n# Check authentication status\nkiro-cli config list\n\n## 🎓 Philosophy Recap\n\nRalph Wiggum is about:\n1. **Iteration**: Repeat until done, not turn-by-turn interaction\n2. **Guardrails**: Add \"signs\" based on failures\n3. **Autonomy**: Let the AI work without constant supervision\n4. **Tuning**: Improve prompts, not blame tools\n5. **Patience**: Trust in eventual consistency\n\n### When to Use Ralph\n\n✅ **Good for:**\n* Large refactoring projects\n* Greenfield development with clear requirements\n* Test-driven development loops\n* Repetitive implementation tasks\n\n❌ **Not ideal for:**\n* Exploratory/research tasks\n* Complex debugging of unknown issues\n* Tasks requiring frequent human judgment\n* High-stakes production changes without review\n\n## 📚 Sources\n* **Original Ralph Wiggum Concept**: [Geoffrey Huntley's Blog][155]\n* **Original Gist**: [Ralph_Wiggum_Loops.md by Mburdo][156]\n* **Kiro CLI Documentation**: [https://kiro.dev/docs/cli/][157]\n* **Kiro CLI Installation**: [https://kiro.dev/cli/][158]\n\n## 🤝 Contributing\n\nImprovements welcome! Please submit issues or pull requests.\n\n## 📄 License\n\nMIT License - Based on the original Ralph Wiggum workflow by Geoffrey Huntley\n\n## About\n\nRalph bedrock\n\n### Resources\n\n[ Readme ][159]\n\n### License\n\n[ View license ][160]\n\n### Contributing\n\n[ Contributing ][161]\n\n### Uh oh!\n\n\nThere was an error while loading. Ask 40-70 questions across multiple categories to gather complete requirements. ## Process 1. **Ask the user what they want to build** - Get their initial project description 2. **Begin structured questioning** - Ask 3-5 questions at a time - Wait for answers before asking more - Cover all categories systematically 3. **Track everything in clarify-session.md** - Create the file if it doesn't exist - Update it after each round of questions - Structure: Questions Asked, Answers Received, Emerging Requirements ## Question Categories to Cover Work through these systematically (not all at once): ### Core Requirements (Priority 1) - What must this do? What's the minimum viable version? - What's explicitly out of scope? - What defines success for this project? ### Users & Context (Priority 1) - Who will use this? What's their technical skill level? - What environment will this run in? - What constraints exist (time, resources, compatibility)? ### Technical Choices (Priority 2) - What programming language? - What framework or libraries? - What database or storage? - Any required dependencies or integrations? ### Integration Points (Priority 2) - What existing systems does this connect to? - What data flows in and out? - What APIs or services are involved? ### Edge Cases (Priority 3) - What happens when things go wrong? - What are the boundary conditions? - How should errors be handled? ### Quality Attributes (Priority 3) - Performance requirements? - Security considerations? - Scalability needs? - Monitoring/logging requirements? ### Existing Patterns (Priority 3) - How do similar things work in this codebase? - What conventions should we follow? - Any existing code to reference? ### Preferences (Priority 4) - Any strong opinions on approach? - Tradeoffs: simplicity vs flexibility vs performance? - Any technologies to avoid? ## Question Design Best Practices - Ask concrete, specific questions - Provide example options when helpful - Group related questions together - Avoid yes/no questions - ask for details - Build on previous answers - Ask \"why\" to understand priorities ## Progress Tracking After every 10 questions, tell the user: - How many questions asked so far - Which categories are covered - Estimated questions remaining ## Completion Criteria Stop asking questions when: - You've covered all 8 categories above - You've asked follow-up questions on complex answers - You genuinely cannot think of more meaningful questions - The user says \"enough\" or \"let's move on\" Typical sessions: 40-70 questions ## When Done 1. [ ] [Task description] - [ ] **HARD STOP** - Review before continuing to low priority ## Low Priority / Nice-to-Have - [ ] [Optional enhancement] --- ## Completed (Completed tasks will be moved here) ``` ## Task Writing Rules 1. **Be Specific**: - Bad: \"Add authentication\" - Good: \"Add JWT authentication middleware to /api routes with token validation\" 2. **Be Measurable**: - Include success criteria when possible - \"Create REST endpoint /api/users that returns 200 with user list\" 3. **One Thing Per Task**: - Split compound tasks - \"Set up database\" → Multiple tasks for connection, migrations, models 4. **Include Context**: - Reference specific files/functions when known - \"Update UserController.js to add password hashing with bcrypt\" 5. **Order by Dependency**: - Tasks that enable others come first - Setup → Core features → Enhancements → Polish ## HARD STOP Placement Insert `**HARD STOP**` markers at natural review points: - After core infrastructure is set up - After MVP/vertical slice is complete - Before moving to lower priority items - At any point requiring human verification These stops allow the user to review progress and adjust priorities. ## Vertical Slice Approach (Recommended) When structuring the Critical section, prefer delivering one complete end-to-end flow first: ```markdown ## Critical (Vertical Slice - Simplest Complete Flow) - [ ] Set up project structure (package.json, dependencies, folder structure) - [ ] Create simplest happy path implementation end-to-end - [ ] Add basic test for the happy path - [ ] **HARD STOP** - Verify slice works before expanding ``` This ensures something works fully before building more. ## Project-Specific Guardrails Based on the requirements, add specific guardrails to PROMPT.md: - If using a specific testing framework: \"Run `npm test` after changes\" - If security is critical: \"Never commit secrets or API keys\" - If performance matters: \"Profile code and ensure <100ms response time\" - If specific patterns exist: \"Follow the repository's [pattern] convention\" ## After Generation Tell the user: ``` ✅ Generated execution files: - PROMPT.md: [X] words, [Y] guardrails - TODO.md: [Z] tasks across [N] priority levels Review these files and adjust if needed. When ready to execute: # Basic loop while :; do cat PROMPT.md | kiro-cli chat --no-interactive -a; done # Safe loop with max iterations (recommended) See README.md for safe loop examples with iteration limits ``` ## Important Notes - Keep PROMPT.md concise - long prompts lead to confusion - Make tasks small enough to complete in one iteration - Add enough guardrails to prevent common mistakes - The execution phase will iterate many times, so clarity is key tools: - file_operations - read_file - write_file - edit_file settings: temperature: 0.5 max_tokens: 4000\n"

Should we retry, queue, or alert?\" - \"Would you prefer approach A (faster but less flexible) or B (more complex but extensible)?\" - \"Walk me through the exact flow when a user does X\" - \"What are your latency requirements for this operation?\" - \"Who should have access to this? MAINTAIN RUNNING DRAFT After every 2-3 questions, update the draft spec file with accumulated information. This ensures nothing is lost if the session is interrupted. ### 4. BE ADAPTIVE Base your next question on previous answers. If the user mentions something interesting, probe deeper. Do not follow a rigid script. ## QUESTION CATEGORIES TO COVER Work through these systematically during the interview: ### Scope Definition - What is explicitly OUT of scope for this implementation? - What's the MVP vs. full vision? Where do we draw the line? - Are there related features we should NOT touch? - What should Ralph ignore even if it seems relevant? ### User Stories (CRITICAL - get this right) - Break the feature into discrete user stories (US-1, US-2, etc.) - Each story MUST be completable in ONE focused coding session - If a story sounds too big, ask: \"Can we break this into smaller stories?\" - For each story, get VERIFIABLE acceptance criteria: - BAD: \"Works correctly\", \"Is fast\", \"Handles errors well\" - GOOD: \"Returns 200 for valid input\", \"Shows error message for invalid email\", \"Response < 200ms\" - Ask: \"How would you verify this story is complete? Continue until user signals completion ### Finalization (When User Says \"Done\") When the user says \"done\", \"finalize\", \"finished\", \"that's all\", \"complete\", or \"wrap up\": 1. STOP IMMEDIATELY - do not offer to implement or suggest next steps ## FILE FORMATS ### Draft Spec (.kiro/lisa-draft.md) ```markdown # Specification Draft: {Feature Name} *Interview in progress - Started: {timestamp}* ## Overview [Brief description of the feature] ## Problem Statement [What problem does this solve?] ## Scope ### In Scope - -f \"$PROMPT_FILE\" ]; then echo -e \"${RED}Error: $PROMPT_FILE not found${NC}\" echo \"Make sure you've run the plan phase first.\" exit 1 fi # Check if kiro-cli is installed if ! command -v kiro-cli &> /dev/null; then echo -e \"${RED}Error: kiro-cli not found${NC}\" echo \"Please install Kiro CLI: https://kiro.dev/cli/\" exit 1 fi # Initialize log file echo \"Ralph Execution Log - $(date)\" > \"$LOG_FILE\" echo \"Max Iterations: $MAX_ITERATIONS\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" echo \"Completion Word: $COMPLETION_WORD\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" echo \"Prompt File: $PROMPT_FILE\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" echo \"----------------------------------------\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" echo \"\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" # Display banner echo -e \"${BLUE}╔════════════════════════════════════════════════╗${NC}\" echo -e \"${BLUE}║ Ralph Wiggum Execution Loop - Kiro ║${NC}\" echo -e \"${BLUE}╚════════════════════════════════════════════════╝${NC}\" echo \"\" echo -e \"${YELLOW}Configuration:${NC}\" echo -e \" Max Iterations: ${GREEN}$MAX_ITERATIONS${NC}\" echo -e \" Completion Word: ${GREEN}$COMPLETION_WORD${NC}\" echo -e \" Prompt File: ${GREEN}$PROMPT_FILE${NC}\" echo -e \" Log File: ${GREEN}$LOG_FILE${NC}\" echo \"\" echo -e \"${YELLOW}Press Ctrl+C to stop the loop at any time${NC}\" echo \"\" # Confirmation read -p \"Ready to start execution? (y/n) \" -n 1 -r echo \"\" if [[ ! $REPLY =~ ^[Yy]$ ]]; then echo -e \"${YELLOW}Execution cancelled${NC}\" exit 0 fi # Main execution loop iteration=0 start_time=$(date +%s) echo -e \"${GREEN}Starting execution loop...${NC}\" echo \"\" while [ $iteration -lt $MAX_ITERATIONS ]; do iteration=$((iteration + 1)) iteration_start=$(date +%s) echo -e \"${BLUE}━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━${NC}\" echo -e \"${BLUE}Iteration $iteration/$MAX_ITERATIONS${NC}\" echo -e \"${BLUE}━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━${NC}\" # Log iteration start echo \"=== Iteration $iteration - $(date) ===\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" # Execute kiro-cli with the prompt output=$(cat \"$PROMPT_FILE\" | kiro-cli chat --no-interactive -a 2>&1) || { echo -e \"${RED}Error: kiro-cli command failed${NC}\" echo \"$output\" echo \"ERROR: kiro-cli failed at iteration $iteration\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" echo \"$output\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" exit 1 } # Display output echo \"$output\" # Log output echo \"$output\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" echo \"\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" # Check for completion if echo \"$output\" | grep -q \"$COMPLETION_WORD\"; then iteration_end=$(date +%s) iteration_time=$((iteration_end - iteration_start)) total_time=$((iteration_end - start_time)) echo \"\" echo -e \"${GREEN}╔════════════════════════════════════════════════╗${NC}\" echo -e \"${GREEN}║ TASK COMPLETED! 🎉 ║${NC}\" echo -e \"${GREEN}╚════════════════════════════════════════════════╝${NC}\" echo \"\" echo -e \"${YELLOW}Summary:${NC}\" echo -e \" Completed at iteration: ${GREEN}$iteration${NC}\" echo -e \" Total iterations: ${GREEN}$iteration${NC}\" echo -e \" Total time: ${GREEN}$((total_time / 60))m $((total_time % 60))s${NC}\" echo -e \" Log file: ${GREEN}$LOG_FILE${NC}\" echo \"\" # Log completion echo \"=== COMPLETED at iteration $iteration ===\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" echo \"Total time: $total_time seconds\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" exit 0 fi # Check if we're stuck (same error repeated) if echo \"$output\" | grep -q \"STUCK\"; then echo \"\" echo -e \"${RED}╔════════════════════════════════════════════════╗${NC}\" echo -e \"${RED}║ EXECUTION STUCK ║${NC}\" echo -e \"${RED}╚════════════════════════════════════════════════╝${NC}\" echo \"\" echo -e \"${YELLOW}The agent reported being stuck. Breaking down the current task in TODO.md\" echo -e \" 3. Fixing the issue manually and resuming\" echo \"\" echo \"=== STUCK at iteration $iteration ===\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" exit 1 fi # Calculate iteration time iteration_end=$(date +%s) iteration_time=$((iteration_end - iteration_start)) echo -e \"${YELLOW}Iteration time: ${iteration_time}s${NC}\" echo \"\" # Small delay to avoid rate limiting sleep 2 done # Max iterations reached total_time=$(($(date +%s) - start_time)) echo \"\" echo -e \"${YELLOW}╔════════════════════════════════════════════════╗${NC}\" echo -e \"${YELLOW}║ MAX ITERATIONS REACHED ║${NC}\" echo -e \"${YELLOW}╚════════════════════════════════════════════════╝${NC}\" echo \"\" echo -e \"${YELLOW}Summary:${NC}\" echo -e \" Iterations completed: ${GREEN}$MAX_ITERATIONS${NC}\" echo -e \" Total time: ${GREEN}$((total_time / 60))m $((total_time % 60))s${NC}\" echo -e \" Log file: ${GREEN}$LOG_FILE${NC}\" echo \"\" echo -e \"${YELLOW}Next steps:${NC}\" echo -e \" 1. Review TODO.md to see progress\" echo -e \" 2. Check git log to see what was completed\" echo -e \" 3. Adjust PROMPT.md if needed\" echo -e \" 4. Run again with: ./ralph-execute.sh\" echo \"\" echo \"=== MAX ITERATIONS REACHED ===\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" echo \"Total time: $total_time seconds\" >> \"$LOG_FILE\" exit 1\n"

Implement completely\n  5. Run tests\n  6. Fix failures before continuing\n  7. Mark complete in TODO.md\n  8. Commit: `git add -A && git commit -m \"descriptive message\"`\n  9. Continue to next task\n  ## Signs (Guardrails)\n  - Always read files before editing\n  - Never skip failing tests\n  - If tests fail 3 times on same issue, output: STUCK - [describe issue]\n  - Don't refactor unrelated code\n  - Keep changes focused\n  - Update TODO.md immediately\n  ## Completion\n  When all tasks in TODO.md are marked `[x]` and all tests pass, output: DONE\n  ```\n\n  ## TODO.md Structure\n  ```markdown\n  # TODO\n  ## Critical (MVP - Must Complete)\n  - [ ] [Specific, measurable task]\n  - [ ] **HARD STOP** - Verify core flow works end-to-end\n  ## High Priority\n  - [ ] [Task]\n  ## Medium Priority\n  - [ ] [Task]\n  - [ ] **HARD STOP** - Review before continuing\n  ## Low Priority / Nice-to-Have\n  - [ ] [Task]\n  ---\n  ## Completed\n  ```\n\n  ## Task Writing Rules\n  1. Be Specific (not \"Add auth\" but \"Add JWT middleware to /api routes\")\n  2. Be Measurable (include success criteria)\n  3. One Thing Per Task\n  4. Include Context (reference specific files/functions)\n  5. PROMPT.md - Instructions for the execution phase\n  2. TODO.md - Prioritized task checklist\n\n  ## Process\n  1. **Read clarify-session.md** - Understand all requirements\n  2. **Generate PROMPT.md** (~150-300 words, focused, actionable, with guardrails, \"DONE\" on completion)\n  3. **Generate TODO.md** (prioritized tasks, HARD STOP checkpoints, dependency-ordered)\n  4. **Validate** - Ensure all requirements covered\n\n  ## PROMPT.md Structure\n  ```markdown\n  # PROMPT.md\n  ## Project\n  [One-line description]\n  ## Requirements\n  Read clarify-session.md for full requirements. Key points:\n  - [Critical requirements]\n  ## Instructions\n  1. Read TODO.md for current tasks\n  2. Pick highest priority incomplete task\n  3. Read files before editing\n  4. Implement completely\n  5. Run tests\n  6. Fix failures before continuing\n  7. Mark complete in TODO.md\n  8. Commit: `git add -A && git commit -m \"descriptive message\"`\n  9. Continue to next task\n  ## Signs (Guardrails)\n  - Always read files before editing\n  - Never skip failing tests\n  - If tests fail 3 times on same issue, output: STUCK - [describe issue]\n  - Don't refactor unrelated code\n  - Keep changes focused\n  - Update TODO.md immediately\n  ## Completion\n  When all tasks in TODO.md are marked `[x]` and all tests pass, output: DONE\n  ```\n\n  ## TODO.md Structure\n  ```markdown\n  # TODO\n  ## Critical (MVP - Must Complete)\n  - [ ] [Specific, measurable task]\n  - [ ] **HARD STOP** - Verify core flow works end-to-end\n  ## High Priority\n  - [ ] [Task]\n  ## Medium Priority\n  - [ ] [Task]\n  - [ ] **HARD STOP** - Review before continuing\n  ## Low Priority / Nice-to-Have\n  - [ ] [Task]\n  ---\n  ## Completed\n  ```\n\n  ## Task Writing Rules\n  1. Ralph does.**\n\n  ## Context\n  This project is the **mineral-exploration-machine-learning-public-paper-check** tool.\n  It scans GitHub repositories (primarily RichardScottOZ/mineral-exploration-machine-learning) to extract paper/report/thesis links, stores them in SQLite, and checks their public accessibility using Playwright browser automation.\n\n  Existing modules: models.py, database.py, checker.py, readme_parser.py, cli.py\n  Tech stack: Python 3.8+, Playwright, SQLite, Click CLI, BeautifulSoup, requests\n\n  ## Your Mission\n  Conduct an in-depth specification interview for a feature, asking probing questions to generate a complete, implementable specification.\n\n  ## CRITICAL RULES\n\n  ### 1. ASK NON-OBVIOUS QUESTIONS\n  NOT: \"What should this feature do?\"\n  INSTEAD: \"How should X interact with the existing Y system?\" / \"What happens when Z fails?\"\n\n  ### 2. CONTINUE UNTIL USER SAYS STOP\n  Interview continues until user says \"done\", \"finalize\", \"finished\", or similar.\n\n  ### 3. MAINTAIN RUNNING DRAFT\n  After every 2-3 questions, update `.kiro/lisa-draft.md`.\n\n  ### 4. BE ADAPTIVE\n  Base next question on previous answers. Probe deeper on interesting mentions.\n\n  ## QUESTION CATEGORIES\n  - Scope Definition (in/out of scope, MVP vs full vision)\n  - User Stories (discrete, completable in one session, with VERIFIABLE acceptance criteria)\n  - Technical Implementation (data models, APIs, integration, error handling)\n  - User Experience (flows, edge cases, error messaging)\n  - Trade-offs and Concerns (performance, security, scalability)\n  - Implementation Phases (2-4 incremental phases)\n  - Verification & Feedback Loops (test commands, success/failure indicators)\n\n  ## WORKFLOW\n\n  ### Initialization\n  1. Extract feature name, create slug\n  2. Create `.kiro/lisa-draft.md` and `.kiro/lisa-{slug}.md`\n  3. Ask first NON-OBVIOUS question\n\n  ### During Interview\n  1. Listen, update draft every 2-3 answers\n  2. Ask next probing question based on answer\n  3. Continue until user signals completion\n\n  ### Finalization (when user says \"done\")\n  1. Write final spec to `docs/specs/{slug}.md`\n  2. Write JSON spec to `docs/specs/{slug}.json`\n  3. Write progress file to `docs/specs/{slug}-progress.txt`\n  4. Delete state file\n  5. Output: \"✅ SPEC COMPLETE\"\n  6.
